{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /logs/agent/sessions/skills/enterprise-artifact-search\n\n# Enterprise Artifact Search Skill (Robust)\n\nThis skill delegates **multi-hop artifact retrieval + structured entity extraction** to a lightweight subagent, keeping the main agent’s context lean.\n\nIt is designed for datasets where a workspace contains many interlinked artifacts (documents, chat logs, meeting transcripts, PRs, URLs) plus reference metadata (employee/customer directories).\n\nThis version adds two critical upgrades:\n1) **Product grounding & anti-distractor filtering** (prevents mixing CoFoAIX/other products when asked about CoachForce).\n2) **Key reviewer extraction rules** (prevents “meeting participants == reviewers” mistake; prefers explicit reviewers, then evidence-based contributors).\n\n---\n\n## When to Invoke This Skill\n\nInvoke when ANY of the following is true:\n\n1. The question requires **multi-hop** evidence gathering (artifact → references → other artifacts).\n2. The answer must be **retrieved** from artifacts (IDs/names/dates/roles), not inferred.\n3. Evidence is scattered across multiple artifact types (docs + slack + meetings + PRs + URLs).\n4. You need **precise pointers** (doc_id/message_id/meeting_id/pr_id) to justify outputs.\n5. You must keep context lean and avoid loading large files into context.\n\n---\n\n## Why Use This Skill?\n\n**Without this skill:** you manually grep many files, risk missing cross-links, and often accept the first “looks right” report (common failure: wrong product).\n\n**With this skill:** a subagent:\n- locates candidate artifacts fast\n- follows references across channels/meetings/docs/PRs\n- extracts structured entities (employee IDs, doc IDs)\n- **verifies product scope** to reject distractors\n- returns a compact evidence map with artifact pointers\n\nTypical context savings: **70–95%**.\n\n---\n\n## Invocation\n\nUse this format:\n\n```python\nTask(subagent_type=\"enterprise-artifact-search\", prompt=\"\"\"\nDataset root: /root/DATA\nQuestion: <paste the question verbatim>\n\nOutput requirements:\n- Return JSON-ready extracted entities (employee IDs, doc IDs, etc.).\n- Provide evidence pointers: artifact_id(s) + short supporting snippets.\n\nConstraints:\n- Avoid oracle/label fields (ground_truth, gold answers).\n- Prefer primary artifacts (docs/chat/meetings/PRs/URLs) over metadata-only shortcuts.\n- MUST enforce product grounding: only accept artifacts proven to be about the target product.\n\"\"\")\n```\n\n---\n\n## Core Procedure (Must Follow)\n\n### Step 0 — Parse intent + target product\n- Extract:\n  - target product name (e.g., “CoachForce”)\n  - entity types needed (e.g., author employee IDs, key reviewer employee IDs)\n  - artifact types likely relevant (“Market Research Report”, docs, review threads)\n\nIf product name is missing in question, infer cautiously from nearby context ONLY if explicitly supported by artifacts; otherwise mark AMBIGUOUS.\n\n---\n\n### Step 1 — Build candidate set (wide recall, then filter)\nSearch in this order:\n1) Product artifact file(s): `/root/DATA/products/<Product>.json` if exists.\n2) Global sweep (if needed): other product files and docs that mention the product name.\n3) Within found channels/meetings: follow doc links (e.g., `/archives/docs/<doc_id>`), referenced meeting chats, PR mentions.\n\nCollect all candidates matching:\n- type/document_type/title contains “Market Research Report” (case-insensitive)\n- OR doc links/slack text contains “Market Research Report”\n- OR meeting transcripts tagged document_type “Market Research Report”\n\n---\n\n### Step 2 — HARD Product Grounding (Anti-distractor gate)\nA candidate report is **VALID** only if it passes **at least 2 independent grounding signals**:\n\n**Grounding signals (choose any 2+):**\nA) Located under the correct product artifact container (e.g., inside `products/CoachForce.json` *and* associated with that product’s planning channels/meetings).\nB) Document content/title explicitly mentions the target product name (“CoachForce”) or a canonical alias list you derive from artifacts.\nC) Shared in a channel whose name is clearly for the target product (e.g., `planning-CoachForce`, `#coachforce-*`) OR a product-specific meeting series (e.g., `CoachForce_planning_*`).\nD) The document id/link path contains a product-specific identifier consistent with the target product (not another product).\nE) A meeting transcript discussing the report includes the target product context in the meeting title/series/channel reference.\n\n**Reject rule (very important):**\n- If the report content repeatedly names a different product (e.g., “CoFoAIX”) and lacks CoachForce grounding → mark as DISTRACTOR and discard, even if it is found in the same file or near similar wording.\n\n**Why:** Benchmarks intentionally insert same doc type across products; “first hit wins” is a common failure.\n\n---\n\n### Step 3 — Select the correct report version\nIf multiple VALID reports exist, choose the “final/latest” by this precedence:\n\n1) Explicit “latest” marker (id/title/link contains `latest`, or most recent date field)\n2) Explicit “final” marker\n3) Otherwise, pick the most recent by `date` field\n4) If dates missing, choose the one most frequently referenced in follow-up discussions (slack replies/meeting chats)\n\nKeep the selected report’s doc_id and link as the anchor.\n\n---\n\n### Step 4 — Extract author(s)\nExtract authors in this priority order:\n1) Document fields: `author`, `authors`, `created_by`, `owner`\n2) PR fields if the report is introduced via PR: `author`, `created_by`\n3) Slack: the user who posted “Here is the report…” message (only if it clearly links to the report doc_id and is product-grounded)\n\nNormalize into **employee IDs**:\n- If already an `eid_*`, keep it.\n- If only a name appears, resolve via employee directory metadata (name → employee_id) but only after you have product-grounded evidence.\n\n---\n\n### Step 5 — Extract key reviewers (DO NOT equate “participants” with reviewers)\nKey reviewers must be **evidence-based contributors**, not simply attendees.\n\nUse this priority order:\n\n**Tier 1 (best): explicit reviewer fields**\n- Document fields: `reviewers`, `key_reviewers`, `approvers`, `requested_reviewers`\n- PR fields: `reviewers`, `approvers`, `requested_reviewers`\n\n**Tier 2: explicit feedback authors**\n- Document `feedback` sections that attribute feedback to specific people/IDs\n- Meeting transcripts where turns are attributable to people AND those people provide concrete suggestions/edits\n\n**Tier 3: slack thread replies to the report-share message**\n- Only include users who reply with substantive feedback/suggestions/questions tied to the report.\n- Exclude:\n  - the author (unless question explicitly wants them included as reviewer too)\n  - pure acknowledgements (“looks good”, “thanks”) unless no other reviewers exist\n\n**Critical rule:**\n- Meeting `participants` list alone is NOT sufficient.\n  - Only count someone as a key reviewer if the transcript shows they contributed feedback\n  - OR they appear in explicit reviewer fields.\n\nIf the benchmark expects “key reviewers” to be “the people who reviewed in the review meeting”, then your evidence must cite the transcript lines/turns that contain their suggestions.\n\n---\n\n### Step 6 — Validate IDs & de-duplicate\n- All outputs must be valid employee IDs (pattern `eid_...`) and exist in the employee directory if provided.\n- Remove duplicates while preserving order:\n  1) authors first\n  2) key reviewers next\n\n---\n\n## Output Format (Strict, JSON-ready)\n\nReturn:\n\n### 1) Final Answer Object\n```json\n{\n  \"target_product\": \"<ProductName>\",\n  \"report_doc_id\": \"<doc_id>\",\n  \"author_employee_ids\": [\"eid_...\"],\n  \"key_reviewer_employee_ids\": [\"eid_...\"],\n  \"all_employee_ids_union\": [\"eid_...\"]\n}\n```\n\n### 2) Evidence Map (pointers + minimal snippets)\nFor each extracted ID, include:\n- artifact type + artifact id (doc_id / meeting_id / slack_message_id / pr_id)\n- a short snippet that directly supports the mapping\n\nExample evidence record:\n```json\n{\n  \"employee_id\": \"eid_xxx\",\n  \"role\": \"key_reviewer\",\n  \"evidence\": [\n    {\n      \"artifact_type\": \"meeting_transcript\",\n      \"artifact_id\": \"CoachForce_planning_2\",\n      \"snippet\": \"…Alex: We should add a section comparing CoachForce to competitor X…\"\n    }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Recommendation Types\n\nReturn one of:\n- **USE_EVIDENCE** — evidence sufficient and product-grounded\n- **NEED_MORE_SEARCH** — missing reviewer signals; must expand search (PRs, slack replies, other meetings)\n- **AMBIGUOUS** — conflicting product signals or multiple equally valid reports\n\n---\n\n## Common Failure Modes (This skill prevents them)\n\n1) **Cross-product leakage**  \nPicking “Market Research Report” for another product (e.g., CoFoAIX) because it appears first.  \n→ Fixed by Step 2 (2-signal product grounding).\n\n2) **Over-inclusive reviewers**  \nTreating all meeting participants as reviewers.  \n→ Fixed by Step 5 (evidence-based reviewer definition).\n\n3) **Wrong version**  \nChoosing draft over final/latest.  \n→ Fixed by Step 3.\n\n4) **Schema mismatch**  \nReturning a flat list when evaluator expects split fields.  \n→ Fixed by Output Format.\n\n---\n\n## Mini Example (Your case)\n\nQuestion:  \n“Find employee IDs of the authors and key reviewers of the Market Research Report for the CoachForce product?”\n\nCorrect behavior:\n- Reject any report whose content/links are clearly about CoFoAIX unless it also passes 2+ CoachForce grounding signals.\n- Select CoachForce’s final/latest report.\n- Author from doc field `author`.\n- Key reviewers from explicit `reviewers/key_reviewers` if present; else from transcript turns or slack replies showing concrete feedback.\n\n---\n\n## Do NOT Invoke When\n\n- The answer is in a single small known file and location with no cross-references.\n- The task is a This market research report delves into the current market landscape, target audience, competitive analysis, and potential growth opportunities for CoFoAIX. The report aims to provide insights that will guide strategic decisions and marketing efforts to maximize the product's success in the market.\\nMarket Overview: The market for AI-powered sales coaching tools is rapidly expanding, driven by the increasing demand for enhanced sales performance and efficiency. As businesses strive to gain a competitive edge, the adoption of AI technologies in sales processes is becoming more prevalent. The global market for AI in sales is projected to grow significantly over the next five years, with a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of approximately 25%. This growth is fueled by advancements in AI technology, increased focus on customer experience, and the need for data-driven decision-making.\\nTarget Audience: CoFoAIX primarily targets sales teams within medium to large enterprises across various industries, including technology, finance, healthcare, and retail. The ideal users are sales managers and representatives who are looking to improve their sales techniques, close rates, and overall performance. Additionally, companies with a strong focus on digital transformation and innovation are likely to be early adopters of AI-powered sales coaching solutions.\\nCompetitive Analysis: The competitive landscape for AI-powered sales coaching tools includes several key players such as Gong, Chorus.ai, and SalesLoft. These competitors offer similar functionalities, including call recording, transcription, and analytics. However, CoFoAIX differentiates itself by providing real-time feedback during sales interactions, which is a unique selling proposition. Additionally, CoFoAIX's user-friendly interface and seamless integration with existing CRM systems give it a competitive edge in the market.\\nProduct Features: CoFoAIX offers a range of features designed to enhance sales performance. Key features include real-time feedback on sales calls and meetings, personalized coaching tips, performance analytics, and integration with popular CRM platforms. The AI engine analyzes conversations to identify areas for improvement, such as communication style, objection handling, and closing techniques. This enables sales reps to make immediate adjustments and improve their effectiveness.\\nMarket Opportunities: There are significant growth opportunities for CoFoAIX in the market. The platform will utilize microservices to ensure flexibility and scalability, with each service handling specific functionalities such as NLP processing, data analytics, and user management.\\nMachine Learning and NLP: The platform will leverage advanced Natural Language Processing (NLP) and machine learning technologies to provide actionable insights during sales interactions. This market research report delves into the current market landscape, target audience, competitive analysis, and potential growth opportunities for CoFoAIX. The report aims to provide insights that will guide strategic decisions and marketing efforts to maximize the product's success in the market.\\nMarket Overview: The market for AI-powered sales coaching tools is rapidly expanding, driven by the increasing demand for enhanced sales performance and efficiency. Industry-specific drivers such as the rise of digital transformation in the technology sector, regulatory pressures in finance, and the shift towards telehealth in healthcare are contributing to the adoption of AI technologies in sales processes. As businesses strive to gain a competitive edge, the adoption of AI technologies in sales processes is becoming more prevalent. The global market for AI in sales is projected to grow significantly over the next five years, with a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of approximately 25%. This growth is fueled by advancements in AI technology, increased focus on customer experience, and the need for data-driven decision-making.\\nTarget Audience: CoFoAIX primarily targets sales teams within medium to large enterprises across various industries, including technology, finance, healthcare, and retail. Medium enterprises are defined as companies with 100 to 999 employees, while large enterprises have 1,000 or more employees. The ideal users are sales managers and representatives who are looking to improve their sales techniques, close rates, and overall performance. Additionally, companies with a strong focus on digital transformation and innovation are likely to be early adopters of AI-powered sales coaching solutions. Demographically, these sales teams are often composed of individuals aged 25-45, with a mix of entry-level and experienced sales professionals.\\nCompetitive Analysis: The competitive landscape for AI-powered sales coaching tools includes several key players such as Gong, Chorus.ai, and SalesLoft. These competitors offer similar functionalities, including call recording, transcription, and analytics. However, CoFoAIX differentiates itself by providing real-time feedback during sales interactions, which is a unique selling proposition. A comparison table shows that while Gong and Chorus.ai offer post-call analytics, CoFoAIX's real-time feedback allows for immediate adjustments, enhancing sales effectiveness on the spot. Additionally, CoFoAIX's user-friendly interface and seamless integration with existing CRM systems give it a competitive edge in the market.\\nProduct Features: CoFoAIX offers a range of features designed to enhance sales performance. Key features include real-time feedback on sales calls and meetings, personalized coaching tips, performance analytics, and integration with popular CRM platforms. The real-time feedback mechanism operates by utilizing natural language processing (NLP) and machine learning algorithms to analyze ongoing conversations. It identifies key moments where sales representatives can improve, such as adjusting tone, addressing objections, or optimizing closing techniques. This immediate analysis allows sales reps to make on-the-spot adjustments, thereby improving their effectiveness in real-time.\\nMarket Opportunities: There are significant growth opportunities for CoFoAIX in the market. As remote work and virtual meetings become more common, the demand for tools that enhance virtual sales interactions is increasing. In the healthcare sector, for example, a medical device company using AI-powered tools saw a 40% improvement in virtual sales presentations. Similarly, a financial services firm reported a 25% increase in client acquisition rates after implementing AI-driven sales coaching. Additionally, the growing emphasis on data-driven decision-making in sales processes presents an opportunity for CoFoAIX to position itself as a critical tool for sales optimization. Expanding into international markets and forming strategic partnerships with CRM providers could further enhance market penetration.\\nChallenges and Risks: Despite the promising market potential, CoFoAIX faces challenges such as data privacy concerns, the need for continuous AI model improvements, and potential resistance to change from traditional sales teams. Ensuring compliance with data protection regulations, such as GDPR and CCPA, is critical. CoFoAIX employs robust encryption methods and anonymization techniques to protect user data and maintain privacy. Additionally, maintaining the accuracy and reliability of AI-generated insights is crucial to overcoming these challenges. To address resistance to change, CoFoAIX can implement strategies such as providing comprehensive training sessions, showcasing success stories from early adopters, and offering pilot programs to demonstrate the tool's effectiveness. An example of successful change management can be seen in a financial services firm that gradually integrated AI tools by first introducing them to a small team, gathering feedback, and then scaling up based on positive results.\\nConclusion: CoFoAIX is well-positioned to capitalize on the growing demand for AI-powered sales coaching solutions. By leveraging its unique real-time feedback feature and focusing on user experience, CoFoAIX can differentiate itself from competitors and capture a significant share of the market. The company's commitment to continuous innovation and staying abreast of technological advancements ensures that CoFoAIX remains at the forefront of the industry.
